My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XXI/M/M_00134/index.shtml -- 134/M]] Will you support the fact that, in the Austrian-Czech Declaration on the Beneš Decrees, which you have announced by you, analogous to the German-Czech Declaration of 1997, there is also a critical commitment to the responsibility of Austrians within the Nazi regime as well as to regret the resulting suffering and injustice?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e3352	Please, Mrs. Foreign Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e3367	I would like to point out that Austria's position is, of course, fundamentally different from that of Germany. The Federal Republic of Germany is known in contrast to Austria as a legal successor to the German Empire. Austria was not capable of action as a state under international law between 1938 and 1945. This does not, of course, exclude the fact that individual Austrians were guilty of Nazi crimes, actively supported them or at least silently took note of them. Austria has therefore taken measures of restitution and compensation for NS victims and former slave and forced workers who also benefit Czech citizens. Furthermore, I can say that the German-Czech declaration, which has been made in the meantime, also addresses a historically different situation and is therefore not the right way to take a completely analogy approach. In my opinion, however, the Czech Republic itself has the means and means to see historical injustice, to accept moral responsibility and to end the application of the legal standards on which this injustice is based.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e3627	Please, Mrs. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e3643	The European Commission has presented opinions in the framework of Agenda 2000 on the areas in which political criteria would be applied at the Copenhagen European Council, which was a prerequisite for the opening of accession negotiations. By the decision of the Luxembourg European Council, which then convened bilateral Intergovernmental Conferences in spring 1998, the European Council unanimously confirmed that the Czech Republic, alongside the other countries mentioned above, fulfils the political criteria for accession negotiations. On the basis of these Commission opinions on the applications for accession of the ten Central and Eastern European countries and Cyprus, the Luxembourg European Council considered that the six Luxembourg Group countries have already achieved both the necessary institutional stability and the guarantee of a democratic and rule of law. The situation in the Czech Republic has not changed in terms of meeting Copenhagen's political criteria. Therefore, this is not a question of EU negotiations. It is, however, a bilateral question which, as I have said, will be bilateral.

I was one of the few foreign ministers present in Tokyo itself, and I mentioned this briefly. Within the framework of what we can do to improve the situation even after an end to terrorism in our societies, I have pointed out the importance of dialogue between civilizations and cultures. I have also taken into account the conflict prevention, which was a special issue, and I have mentioned that apart from Afghanistan, Central Asia will be an important issue for me, but also Belarus and Moldova should not be forgotten, because we need to bring these countries closer to the European Union.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e4924	Please answer, Mrs. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e4941	In advance, I would like to make it clear that Commissioner Fischler's statement, as reflected in the media, that in the negotiations with the EU Commission on a transit follow-up system we, that is, Austria itself, had proposed the withdrawal of the 108% rule, is incorrect. I didn't hear Commissioner Fischler in the original tone. That means, I don't know if he's been correctly quoted in the media. However, he was not present at the talks I and the other members of the Federal Government held with Transport Commissioner de Palacio and with enlargement Commissioner Verheugen. In principle, I note that it is not for Austria but for the Commission to submit the proposals. We have had discussions with the Commission with a view to making the best possible contribution to the interests of Austria and, of course, to lobbying. It would be absurd and would contradict any logic without agreeing to the removal of the 108 percent rule, as it is presented. As you know, the Commission has been trying for over a year to enforce the removal of the 108 per cent rule in the existing transit protocol. A proposal for a Commission regulation on this matter was adopted on 5 June. 303 without amendments: 253 votes in the European Parliament were also approved. So far, I believe, the Austrian Government has succeeded in ensuring that this proposal for a regulation has not been voted on by the Transport Councils. Of course, the Commission's representatives, with whom discussions have been held on this issue, have always put the removal of the 108% rule at the top of their demands. Other demands were also the limitation of a transitional arrangement and not necessarily an ecopoint regime on few Alpine corridors or a limit to one year. Of course, the two proceedings pending before the European Court of Justice against the application of the 108% rule, especially in 1999, 2000 and 2001, show that the Commission is fighting vehemently against the 108% rule. Moreover, this is also a clear indication of the consistent attitude of the entire Federal Government in this, as I believe, so important for Austria. Both demands have been able to ward off the affected members of the Federal Government – German Chancellor, Deputy Chancellor, Minister of Transport and I too. The proposal for a regulation now covers the whole federal territory and is limited to three years. Unfortunately, our failure to achieve success in the 108% rule corresponds to the mood in Europe, as the vote mentioned above also shows in the European Parliament, but also in the position of the Member States. However, I do not think this is a reason for a disaster mood, because the ecopoints for themselves are also a reduction by the quotaing of the limit on journeys. Unfortunately, this type of limitation has proven to be the only effective limitation of road freight traffic through Austria over all the years, even when 108 percent have been exceeded. The two cases pending before the European Court of Justice are, as mentioned, the non-application by the Commission of the 108% rule, which I would like to say.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e5131	Mrs. Federal Minister! What is the state of the negotiations for a new road cost directive?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e5146	Please answer, Mrs. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e5161	The Commission intends to complete the internal preparatory work before the Barcelona European Council and to have a framework directive by summer, which lays down for all modes of transport the principles of charging infrastructure use and the structure of charges. It is therefore not only about the road, but also about the rails, the inland waterways and aviation. The aim is to set the level of tariffs in accordance with the White Paper, including external costs and conditions for fair competition between modes of transport. A particularly important aspect for Austria is the question of the so-called sensitive areas. In addition to infrastructure and external costs, it will be possible to remove a surcharge for cross-financing of environmentally friendly modes of transport. In a special working group initiated by me with the Commission, Austria has presented these ideas, and I think it is up to the Commission to put the White Paper's announcements into a proposal for a directive. It is no secret that, as in other transport issues, the same opposite interests exist between Austria as a classical transit country and the majority of the other EU partners, whose main objective is to connect markets to the other Member States. However, as soon as the Commission's views are in place, it will be a matter of letting the Austrian idea into a large part of it.

My question is: [[/PAKT/VG/XXI/M/M_00138/index.shtml -- 138/M]] From your point of view, does the colported integration of the General Secretariat of the Foreign Office into the ministerial office result in a devalue of this institution?
ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e5684	Please answer, Mrs. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e5699	Under the Federal Ministry Act, the Secretary-General is responsible for the summary treatment of agendas in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. The General Secretariat, established up to date, shall assist the Secretary-General in the tasks he is responsible for. Within the framework of the budgetary consolidation requirements imposed on the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the General Secretariat, initially set up as a department, will be set up by 1st. On 1 February 2002 the Secretary-General’s office was re-structured and made a unit directly to the Secretary-General. This saved a planning site. Content responsibilities are not affected, and it is not possible to speak of integration into the ministerial office or of a depreciation.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e6606	Mr President. Mrs. Vice chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, I would like to refer specifically to the amendment of the Sports Promotion Act, and I believe that an improvement in quality in the planning and execution of sports projects is urgently needed. In addition to and in addition to the previous control mechanisms, we are now drawing up a professional body. Economic operators and experts must be required to be involved already in the projecting phase. So it is not as before and as in the Court of Auditors' reports that it is only afterwards that it is criticized and examined, but it must already be helped and co-designed in advance. This change will cover all project phases. Firstly, the planning and development of the project and the precise and timely recording of costs. The development of a concrete list of services is an important basis for drawing up tender documents. Secondly, The implementation and monitoring of the project will be ensured by an independent civil liability body. Thirdly, Sustainable economic use is important for many major projects. It is of no use that we provide only the funds for the construction of a sports facility, but we must also try to ensure that sustainable economic management is possible. Thus, we can prevent higher contributions for the club members and also avoid future clubs. Due to the careful and efficient use of the sports promotion equipment, we can create spaces for important sporting issues. In my view, I call this the most important concern in the near future: Women's and girls' sport initiatives. Women and girls are extremely disadvantaged on almost all sports facilities. Football pitches are used almost exclusively by men and boys. There are far too few qualified trainers. There is a lack of adequate sanitation facilities. Girls and women receive a much lower salary for the same performance. This also applies and especially to the top sports. I therefore suggest that the year 2003 will be declared the "Year of Girl and Women's Sports" and that all those responsible for sports at the federal, regional and municipal levels must take special care of this topic. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] I also count school sports, because it is there that the foundation stone is laid for a positive relationship with sports and movement. We need a movement-oriented offer in kindergarten, school, operation and the immediate residential environment. The goal is clear: We want as many girls and women as possible to be able to pursue their sporting interests and to eliminate the disadvantages of women and girls in sports. The efficient use of the means of sport also makes it possible to increase the promotion of the sport of disabled persons. The Handicapped Sports Association – which has already been mentioned by my predecessor – is a member of the Federal Sport Organisation, but is not taught from this budget. It is already clear to me that the sports associations in the BSO do not wish to divide the previous funds into several members. We also want to avoid endangering or questioning the valuable work of the sports and roof associations. Nevertheless, we demand that the BSO take up the Disabled Sports Association as a full member and that it should also take up the constant annual increases in the means of sport. More and more disabled people want to participate in the sporty life. Sports associations and clubs are also responding to this need. However, the care of disabled athletes is very cost-intensive. I therefore welcome Mrs Vice-Chancellor's rubble action as the first important measure. However, I believe that we will need more money for sports in the future. After reaching the savings goals, I demand more money for sports. More money for sport means more than 100 000 jobs, partly very high-quality jobs, more money for sports means a significant contribution to health care and less follow-up costs in health care. More money for sport means vital and powerful citizens. This also makes a significant contribution to strengthening Austria's economic location. Investments in sport are therefore paying off several times. I think that it is therefore useful to give sport an even greater priority in the future.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e6768	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Westenthaler: Kogler is in the presidency!]] And if you are committed to ensuring that sports matters are always dealt with in such a prominent place, namely at the beginning of the agenda, I will be very pleased and you will, of course, also find our support for this. I do not think it is appropriate to go in the direction of the Austrian Bundesliga in view of the young people's problems we have in Austrian football now, that we say we take even more foreign players into the Austrian Bundesliga. I believe that the ÖFB, but also the Bundesliga clubs, have recognised that the future of Austrian football will only be secured and can only be a positive development if we promote our young Austrian talents and give them the opportunity to play in Austrian clubs. [[It is a pleasure for the liberals and the ÖVP, as well as for representatives of the SPÖ.]] The issue of today's debate, the bill that we are discussing today, is a novel to the Federal Sports Promotion Act, which will significantly improve in connection with sports facilities whose infrastructure and construction will result in in Austria. This amendment provides that the promotion of large investment projects is linked to an opinion by independent auditors on the efficiency, effectiveness and economy of the establishment, and also provides for an accompanying controlling. This is actually a follow-up to the fact that the usual level of control, as is otherwise the case with large projects, should also be introduced there. I would like to say that we are also in line with the Court of Auditors' suggestion, which in the past has always criticised that there are no appropriate control instruments for the award of public funds. We have ensured that this will be the case for sports sites infrastructure construction in Austria in the future. Members of this Controlling Advisory Board or of these Advisory Boards will be: one representative of my ministry and one of the promoters, say: Country and local authorities which also provide funds, as well as an auditor to draw up such an opinion. In practice, we have already implemented this on a voluntary basis with the establishment of the Salzburg Stadium. I would also like to thank the founders and operators of the Salzburg Stadium very much for allowing us to do this pilot project. I was asked at the time: Is that not, as it were, a distrust of the stage judges? – In the course of this project creation, we have identified – both sides, federal government and country and city of Salzburg – that everyone has benefited from the fact that the Controlling Advisory Board has also made positive suggestions for the project and that it also enables us to build it more cost-effectively, which is a great advantage for all sides. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We have done something in the planning of the stadium in Salzburg, which we will take into account in the future in all sports venues, namely to ensure that the establishment and promotion of the federal government for this project is also linked to a young project, because I believe that it is also important in terms of sport policy that we not only build beautiful sports venues, but also that we have young athletes who can play in these sports venues and practice their sport. Therefore, in connection with this promotion of the stadium with the country of Salzburg and with the association Austria Salzburg, we have also initiated an appropriate young project, and this will be done in the future in all other sports venue projects. I believe that young people's work is at all the central point in the field of sports. ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e9342	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Appreciate Mrs. Vice-Chancellor! High house! Around 60 percent of Austrians run sports. The role of sport in maintaining health and leisure activities is becoming increasingly important. You run sports mainly for joy at the movement, as well as to be fit and healthy. 95 percent of active athletes are active in wide and leisure sports, only 5 percent in performance and competition. The mountain bike is an area that is associated with wide-ranging and leisure sports. This sport has experienced strong upswing especially in Austria and in addition to the health aspects, it also generates strong impulses for the local tourism and leisure industry. In the past, conflicts between the property owners and the tourism industry emerged during the exercise of this sport. It was therefore very useful to enter into a regional partnership between the property owners and the leisure industry. In this context, it should not be forgotten that there are around 108 000 kilometres of private forest roads in Austria, of which about 16 000 kilometres are already known as mountain bike routes. These regionally concluded partnerships would be ad absurd with the proposal from the SPÖ to release all forestry routes from a width of 1.5 meters for mountain bikers. This unimagined action would endanger the already implemented professional design of mountain bike routes. Not only the hunters, but also the hikers who seek relaxation in the forest have little joy in it, would always and everywhere as if from bright sky the cyclists sat around their ears. Since some mountain bike projects are in progress in my district, I have conducted personal discussions with property owners as well as representatives from the tourism industry. Conclusion: Private and regional solutions should be preferred to the general opening of forestry roads. In this context, the regulation for bike routes should also respect forest and wildlife protection. Wildlife protection areas where mountain biking can be temporarily restricted should be accepted by the animals. Hundreds of jobs were created in the course of the market-economic voluntary organisation of mountain bike routes and cycle routes: Route planners, painters, map designers, cycle gastronomy, alpine huts and tourism consulting. The Ministry of Sports has now prepared an application to support efforts in terms of the partnership development of the Austrian cycle paths. The aim is to develop, as far as possible, all suitable forest roads for mountain biking, and to conclude contracts with the owners of the land, which, of course, also regulate liability risks. It is not the legal opening that should be sought, but the reasonable contractual arrangements that allow for the design of conflicts as far as possible. Austria is the discovery of the new century for mountain bikers: Hardly anywhere else is one so open to these health athletes, hardly anywhere else you are so well equipped for their needs, and nowhere else there are better mountain bike trails. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e9580	Mr President. Mrs. Vice chancellor! My very much appreciated Ladies and gentlemen! The issue of mountain biking has been with us in Parliament for a number of years now. I would like to make a few facts on the basic points. We have a total of 108 000 kilometres of forest roads in Austria. By last autumn, there were already contracts for 16 000 kilometres of forest roads, according to which these trails are available to mountain bikers. In the next few weeks a number of routes will be added, so in the spring the mountain bikers will already be available for over 20,000 kilometres of forest roads. In Comparative: The other cycle paths offered by the tourism industry in Austria, such as the Danube cycle path and the cycle paths on the Inn, on the Mur, on the Salzach, total only 10,000 kilometres. This means that there are already twice as many kilometres of forest roads as on other cycle paths available to cyclists. To defend Mr Scheuch – I normally do not need to defend him – I would like to stress that the SPÖ has already changed. I have an issue here of the Social Democratic Parliamentary Group, but from a time when it was still in the government. I would like to read a section of it: "Tourism and transport spokesperson Rudolf Parnigoni highlighted the importance of mountain biking for tourism. The question of the legal position of mountain bikers must be solved as well as that of liability in order to prevent the leavers from moving to South Tyrol. "In addition, we need to expand the range of transport areas for bikers, so that the athletes have an Austrian network of 10,000 to 15 000 kilometres." – In the meantime we have 20 000 kilometers. Further: "A general opening of the forest roads cannot be thought of in the light of the strong right of ownership in the Constitution", says Parnigoni." - Quote. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! It must be said that from climate to Gusenbauer has become a socialist party from a social democratic party. There have obviously been changes here. Who complains that we have too few mountain bike routes available, I show this table about a survey at the 2000 holiday fair. [[The speaker shall keep the table referred to in paragraph 1 up to date.]] Tourism managers from all over Austria were asked if mountain biking was possible in their region. In four federal states – Vorarlberg, Kärnten, Salzburg, Tirol – 100 percent of tourism managers have said that mountain biking is possible in their regions. In Lower Austria, there were 96.8 percent, in Upper Austria 77.8 percent, in Styria 75 percent and in Burgenland 66.7%. To Burgenland you have to say that there are no large forest areas throughout the lake, that is, there are no mountain bike routes available in this region. On the next page on the question: If there are problems with the property owners, the table shows: Even in Burgenland, 100 percent of tourism experts have explained that there is no problem with the property owners. In Vorarlberg and Tyrol this is the same, and in the other countries it is about 90 percent. That is, we have established, on a contractual basis, arrangements for mountain biking to be possible anywhere in Austria. The experts in the Subcommittee also noted this. Even Dipl.-Ing. Christian Hlavac, who was nominated by the Greens as an expert in the Subcommittee, said that there were two possibilities, namely the legal and contractual ones, and both were commonplace. All other experts have voted in favour of the contractual arrangement because we can provide enough areas with the contractual arrangement in Austria. All of this also has administrative follow-up costs, because the forest roads are considered as working areas in the forest in the spatial order. If they are released for traffic – even if only for cyclists – they are subject to road traffic regulations. All forest roads must then be subject to a clearing permit, and there are also very different liability obligations for the forest owner. The studies show that we can go mountain biking anywhere in Austria, and the contractual regulation – our motion for a resolution aims to do so – will ensure that the necessary links are given that we have circular routes. In the future, we will have available the areas necessary for mountain biking on a contractual basis.

However, the government parties are taking into account the survey, because a result of this event here in Parliament was that mobile phones are subject to radiation pollution. Evidence that mobile phones can be harmful to health by, for example, affecting the function of heart pace makers cannot be doubted. That is why a uniform radiation identification of mobile devices is a logical requirement, which the Federal Minister has also accepted, so to speak. She has promised to observe this in this case. With regard to mobile communications facilities, which have repeatedly led to annoyance of the people and to protests, the Federal Minister for Foreign Affairs has provided an opportunity for the adoption of a limit value regulation. But one thing should not be kept silent here: Many demands that the petition contains – and all know that – do not concern the federal legislator. Construction regulations or landscape protection are matters of the countries and communities in which it is not easy to intervene centrally. In the future, it will be important to ensure that in the construction of transmission masts, the arrivals will be informed in good time and will not be surprised at what happened in the past. The Ministry should also assert its influence on operators to increasingly use transmission masts together. Finally, it is necessary to continue research into possible health damage. In the case of new scientific findings, one will then also, but only then, have the law to adapt to these new standards. Mr Firlinger said that it was necessary to keep the right eye in the discussion. I agree with this statement not only with regard to this discussion. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e11213	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen! At the meeting of the Transport Committee on 4 October. I would like to refer to point 2. In point 2, Mrs Bundesminister is asked to influence mobile operators, within the framework of their competence, in order to provide comprehensive information on the surrounding population in advance of the relevant measures when setting up or changing base stations. Dear ladies and gentlemen! It is therefore about information to the affected population. I would like to support the importance of this information by means of an example from my own community. A transmission facility is being built near a retirement home. The mobile operator instructs a construction company to implement this planned facility. A suitable property will be found and a pre-contract will be concluded with the property owner. In the next step, a nature conservation permit will be collected and then the municipality will be contacted. Since the construction project is located outside the local area, construction is not conducted, but it is sufficient for a construction indicator. Legally, therefore, nothing stands in the way of the construction of this transmission system. But now again to the present case: Already at the construction site, that is, before the construction began, the problems occurred – problems caused by pre-construction complaints. These complaints come naturally – you all know that – first of all to the community, and the mayor is also concerned with them. So I was in this area myself. What happened to the arrants, to those affected, the disruptors? It was, as it turns out, only the visual contact to the transmission system and the associated fear that this could cause health impairments to the home residents. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Lichtenberger]] It has also been complained that this transmission system has a negative impact on the location picture. Dear ladies and gentlemen! After these joint intensive discussions with the workers, the operator and the community, a consensus was ultimately reached, in the form of a consensus that everyone concerned sat at a table and agreed that a green belt would be planted next to this transmission system after its completion in order to bring about an optical change. As this example shows, ladies and gentlemen, it is essentially about information and, above all, about timely information. I therefore welcome the agreement between the Austrian Municipal Government and the Mobile Communications Forum, which was reached in August of the previous year. The purpose of this agreement is to ensure the flow of information between the municipalities concerned and the mobile operator. In other words, mobile operators undertake to provide information on planned construction projects in writing and in writing to the relevant community, and the municipalities are of course called to publish these documents and information locally and to start discussions with the locals in advance. Dear ladies and gentlemen! I think that this agreement provides the prerequisite or opportunity for a partnership between all parties involved.

These fears must be taken seriously. There are, of course, fears on the one hand, and on the other hand there are many studies per and counter, but no final proof. I would also like to point out here because it is important to me that we have to ask ourselves how we want to explain this to the Austrians if there is no longer any reception anywhere. – For the population in rural areas in particular, the mobile phone is an additional opportunity and contributes to the upgrading of this area. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Of course, we also have to say – and I would like to stress this here – that Austria has the second largest market penetration in Europe on mobile phones to Norway. I would like to say, because it was interesting for me to quote from a fetter study, where 882 Austrians who have a mobile phone were interviewed: In 1999, half of all households had a mobile phone. In 2002, only over a quarter of households have a mobile phone, most already two, and 7 percent of households have more than four phones! So when we talk about citizens' rights and interests, of course we also need to talk about the need for people to make the phone calls, and we must ensure that we inform them that neighbouring rights are better protected. I would also like many parents to inform their children if they give them a cell phone so that they don't phone continuously, so that they really can also make broadcast breaks – in the truest sense of the word. Children should not call by phone for hours. I think all of these are precautions that can be taken. At this point, I would like to return to an important point in the motion for a resolution, namely that there should be a label for the different phones in the future. It's called "phone" in Austria because it's just -- I just quoted it from the statistics -- it's always at hand and because there's a lot of phone calls in Austria; in all other countries, it's called "mobile phone". We should, I think, really deal sensitively with this issue. We should try to make it possible for the citizen to compare using the instructions for use: How strong is the radiation? Which device do I prefer, which one do I want? Finally, I would like to quote an expert on radiation protection after many experts have already been quoted today, namely the radiation expert Neubauer, who says: "A mobile phone is a near-field transmitter that causes radiation exposure in the head area. Of power, the base station is stronger, only we are normally so far away that the strength of the electromagnetic waves is clearly below that of the mobile phone." – The experts have also said that – I would like to underline this here! And on top of that: "Even if you go on the street, you can be exposed to higher exposures by telephone calls from other users than by base stations." So there are so many opinions on this! There is such a great technical development that we do not need to stand in the way of, but in the light of which we must try to align our laws so as to take account of the needs of the people, as well as to enable the economy and the public to perceive the opportunities associated with it.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-01-31-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00092_d7e12331	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! First of all: We have chosen an abbreviated procedure, not because we do not consider it to be worthy of discussion or because it cannot be done quickly enough, but because all the political groups in this House can clearly identify themselves with the content of this agreement and because we also want to set a sign that a ratification process, as we do in Austria, should lead to the implementation of this agreement throughout the EU. An essential condition for this is that there are trade relations that flourish so that the population has the legitimate hope of increasing their standard of living through economic growth. On the content itself – that is my third comment – I think that we have to look beyond what only economic relations are going on. With this agreement – and this is often underestimated by these association agreements – there is a programmed political dialogue. It is crucial that there are regular meetings between the Foreign Ministers, that there is a very constant discussion at the level of officials, and that even if this is not perfectly fulfilled, this can be addressed in an orderly dialogue. These are precisely the framework conditions that make us hope that the democratic process in the Republic of Croatia will move forward, that the human rights situation can be discussed in a normal dialogue even if there are problems, and that this will lead to a development that, according to a stage plan, Croatia is actually on a rail towards Europe. I am pleased about this and I would also like to say in this context that I am proud that, in the context of the shortened procedure, all political groups have so clearly supported the rapid ratification process. I believe that this is also a very good signal towards an expansion of stability and peace in Europe. - Thank you.

I would therefore like to address just a few essential points. More than 70 bilateral visits took place in Austria in 2001 alone or in Croatia. In the previous year, President Mesić not only made a multi-day official visit to Austria, but also participated in two events in Austria. Discussions also take place on a regular basis at the level of the Heads of Government, the Presidents of Parliament, the Foreign Ministers and numerous other ministers. There are also many contacts at regional and local level, and I myself, of course, intend to travel to Croatia this year and return to my colleague's visit last year. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Since 1955, ladies and gentlemen, Austria has been represented in Agram with a culture facility abroad, and in 2001, that is, last year, 246 events were held by today – as we call it – Zagreb Culture Forum with 108 partners in 36 places in Croatia. The focus here was also on the common Europe and the issues that point towards Europe. However, the excellent bilateral relations are demonstrated, above all, by impressive figures in the field of the economy. The statistics of the Croatian National Bank for the period 1993 to the third quarter of 2001 show Austria with a share of 29.44 percent of total investment as the largest investor in Croatia, followed by the United States and Germany. According to Croatian statistics, Austria is in first place for the first months of 2001 even with impressive 58 percent and Germany in second place. In bilateral trade relations, Austria is fourth in total, with imports and exports respectively at fifth place. In 2002, i.e. this year, it is expected that the one-percent mark of Austrian exports to Croatia will be exceeded. When I think about the tourist balance, I know how much the Austrian Croatia appreciates. Not least last year, Croatia was visited by more than 680 000 Austrian tourists. This makes Austria the only country whose number of visitors exceeds that of 1990. These figures, ladies and gentlemen, prove that the Stabilisation and Association Agreement today, which also includes improving the export and investment framework, is of particular interest to Austria. The EU's Stabilisation and Association Agreement with Croatia, which we are talking about and voting on here today, is – I can say – a milestone in our relations with Croatia. I am pleased that it was possible, and I thank you all for your support.

This means a decline of over 45 000 students, of almost 20 percent.
